      first discipline at every Anishinaabe / Aboriginal-Australian /
      Nyanga claim per the brief's special_instructions and per scope.md
      §"Appropriation protocol"; (10) verifying the named-but-flagged
      discipline at the §I Aboriginal-Australian secondary anchors
      (Radcliffe-Brown 1926, Morphy 1991, Rose 1996, Keen 1994 — none in

      T. Smith, Vizenor — all in sources.yaml); (11) verifying every
      named-historical-figure date claim (Lönnrot 1802-1884; Radcliffe-
      Brown 1926; Hallowell 1960; Vizenor 1989; Smith 1995; Morphy 1991;
      Rose 1996; Keen 1994; Merlan 1998; Siikala 2002; Eugenio 2001;
      Koessler-Ilg 1954; Foerster 1993); (12) verifying the regional-

      (Yingarna, Ngalyod, Wanambi, Wagyl, Yulunggul) against the brief's

    sources_skimmed:
      - >
        Merlan, F., *Caging the Rainbow: Places, Politics, and Aborigines
        in a North Australian Town* (Honolulu: University of Hawaiʻi
        Press, 1998): sources.yaml entry verified as merlan-caging-rainbow.
        Anchor for the §I Rainbow Serpent critique (chs. 1-3 on the
        consolidation-dismantling reading; the regional specificity of
        Yingarna, Ngalyod, Wanambi, Wagyl, Yulunggul; the post-1980s area-
        specialist tradition's reckoning with the recording-history
        situation as itself part of the appropriation problem). PASS on
        attribution.
      - >
        Hallowell, A.I., 'Ojibwa Ontology, Behavior, and World View', in
        *Culture in History: Essays in Honor of Paul Radin*, ed. Stanley
        Diamond (Columbia University Press, 1960), 19-52: sources.yaml
        entry verified as hallowell-ojibwa-world. The "other-than-human
        persons" framework; the Berens River fieldwork of the 1930s in
        northwestern Ontario; the manitouk on the same ontological standing
        as human persons; the relational-and-reciprocity-grounded moral
        framework — all attested in Ch32 claims-approved with the same
        loc-pointers. PASS on attribution.
      - >
        Biebuyck, D. and Mateene, K.C., *The Mwindo Epic from the Banyanga
        (Zaire)* (University of California Press, 1969): sources.yaml
        entry verified as biebuyck-mateene-mwindo. The 1956 Bese recording
        from Shé-Kárisi Candi Rureke at Bese on the upper Oso river in
        the Kivu region of what was then the eastern Belgian Congo; the
        epic's pedagogical apparatus; the killing of Kirimu as hubristic
        over-reach; Nkuba's lightning-seizing as the narrative's own moral
        correction; the celestial-realm trials and humiliations as
        morally-motivated correction; the conditions imposed on Mwindo's
        return to the human world — all attested in Ch29 claims-approved
        with the same loc-pointers. PASS on attribution.
      - >
        Siikala, A.-L., *Mythic Images and Shamanism: A Perspective on
        Kalevala Poetry* (Folklore Fellows' Communications 280, Helsinki:
        Academia Scientiarum Fennica, 2002): sources.yaml entry verified
        as siikala-finnish. The two-layer textual situation (older rune
        material + Lönnrot's 1835 / 1849 *Kalevala* editorial synthesis);
        Lönnrot's 1802-1884 dates and his eleven major collecting tours
        between 1828 and 1844; the Vanha Kalevala (1835) and Uusi Kalevala
        (1849) editions; the older rune material as laconic, fragmentary,
        and not synthesised into a single connected narrative within the
        singing tradition itself; the Iku-Turso connected-episode
        structure as Lönnrot's compositional achievement — all attested
        in Ch31 claims-approved with the same loc-pointers. PASS on
        attribution. Note: Ch41's "Henrik Reinhold Antell" listing among
        the rune-collectors is not corroborated by Ch31 (which lists
        Lönnrot, Europaeus, Ahlqvist, Genetz, the Krohn brothers as the
        principal collectors); see FIND-02 below.
      - >
        Johnston, B., *Ojibway Heritage* (1976/1990): sources.yaml entry
        verified as johnston-ojibway-heritage. Anishinaabe-authored
        insider register of cosmology, manitouk, and the Nanabozho cycle.
        Note: this source is not routed through any EVIDENCE token in
        Ch41 (only mentioned in body prose and inside APPROPRIATION-FLAG
        named_specialists), but it is named correctly in body prose at
        §II per the brief's area-specialist citation requirement.
        Consistent with Ch32 named-specialist precedent. PASS.
      - >
        Johnston, B., *The Manitous: The Spiritual World of the Ojibway*
        (HarperCollins, 1995): sources.yaml entry verified as johnston-
        manitous. Anishinaabe-authored treatment of manitous including
        Mishipeshu and Nanabozho. EVIDENCE-routed at line 116. PASS.
      - >
        Smith, T.S., *The Island of the Anishnaabeg: Thunderers and Water
        Monsters in the Traditional Ojibwe Life-World* (University of
        Idaho Press, 1995): sources.yaml entry verified as smith-island-
        anishnaabeg. The Thunderer / Underwater-manitou dialectic as the
        central interpretive frame; the layered cosmographic equilibrium;
        the necessary-counterpart-not-adversaries reading. EVIDENCE-routed
        at lines 110, 113, 128. Consistent with Ch32 use. PASS.
      - >
        Vizenor, G. (ed.), *Narrative Chance: Postmodern Discourse on
        Native American Indian Literatures* (University of New Mexico
        Press, 1989; reprint Norman: University of Oklahoma Press, 1993):
        sources.yaml entry verified as vizenor-narrative-chance. The
        "Trickster Discourse" essay; the methodological refusal to
        subordinate Anishinaabe trickster narrative to Western combat-myth
        or hero/villain typologies. EVIDENCE-routed at lines 119, 122.
        Consistent with Ch32 use. PASS.
      - >
        Belcher, S., *Epic Traditions of Africa* (Indiana University
        Press, 1999): sources.yaml entry verified as belcher-epic. Ch
        on Mwindo on the maturation-through-humiliation pattern as the
        load-bearing distinguishing feature of the Nyanga material from

      - >
        Eugenio, D.L., *Philippine Folk Literature: The Myths* (University
        of the Philippines Press, 2001): sources.yaml entry verified as
        eugenio-philippine-myths. Bakunawa-and-the-moons sections; the

      - >
        Koessler-Ilg, B., *Cuentan los araucanos: Mitos, leyendas y

      - >
        Foerster González, R., *Introducción a la religiosidad mapuche*

      - >
        Ballentine, D.S., *The Conflict Myth and the Biblical Tradition*

      - >

    references_assumed:
      - >
        sources.yaml entries for A. R. Radcliffe-Brown (1926 *JRAI*
        article on the Rainbow-Serpent myth of Australia), Howard Morphy
        (*Ancestral Connections*, 1991), Deborah Bird Rose (*Nourishing
        Terrains*, 1996), Ian Keen (*Knowledge and Secrecy in an
        Aboriginal Religion*, 1994), Hermann Gunkel (1895 originating
        chaoskampf framing), and Biebuyck's later *Hero and Chief*
        (1978) are NOT in sources.yaml and are correctly handled via
        the named-but-flagged pattern (no EVIDENCE tokens routed through
        them; named in body prose and inside APPROPRIATION-FLAG named_
        specialists fields with the "not currently in sources.yaml" /
        "named-but-flagged in body prose" qualifier). The chapter's
        source overview at line 48 documents the named-but-flagged
        pattern explicitly with a forwarding note for the inventory-
        audit follow-up. Consistent with Ch32 / Ch29 / Ch31 / Ch40
        named-but-flagged precedent.
    quote_pattern_match: >
      The chapter is a synthesis-of-syntheses: it cites no verbatim
      primary-source quotation and no verbatim scholar-quotation,
      instead routing each per-tradition factual claim through the
      same source_id that the corresponding Part-I chapter uses for
      that fact. Verification standard is therefore "does the cited
      Part-I-anchor source_id support the specific claim made", not
      "does the verbatim quotation appear at the specified loc". The
      Part-I claims-approved files were used as the second-order
      verification ground (their factchecks were already passed at
      the chapter-claims stage). Of the 64 EVIDENCE tokens audited,
      63 route through Part-I anchor source_ids that are documented
      to support the specific claim; 1 raises a date-drift finding
      (FIND-01) and 1 raises a name-attestation finding (FIND-02 —
      "Henrik Reinhold Antell" listed in body prose at line 178 has
      no upstream attestation in Ch31 or in sources.yaml, neither
      via EVIDENCE token nor via secondary-anchor named-but-flagged
      apparatus).
    internal_variance_vs_contamination: >
      No cross-tradition category-import contamination findings (in
      contrast to Ch40 FIND-01's "wak'a-rich Sahelian landscape"
      drift): the chapter's per-tradition vocabulary is kept tradition-
      internal throughout (Aboriginal-Australian regional ancestral
      beings stay in Aboriginal-Australian frame; Anishinaabe manitouk
      and Thunderers stay in Anishinaabe frame; Nyanga Nkuba and
      Mwindo stay in Nyanga frame; Iku-Turso and Lönnrot stay in
      Finnic frame; Bakunawa stays in Visayan/Bikol frame). One date
      drift (Foerster 1995 should be 1993; FIND-01) and one name-
      attestation issue (Antell among the rune-collectors;
      FIND-02). Otherwise every per-tradition claim routes through
      the area-specialist anchor for that tradition; the named-but-
      flagged pattern is correctly applied for the §I Aboriginal-
      Australian secondary anchors (Radcliffe-Brown, Morphy, Rose,
      Keen) and the methodological-historical anchors (Gunkel).

tallies:
  supported: 51                # of 53 paragraph-claims
  internal_variance: 0
  unverified: 1                # FIND-02 Antell name-attestation
  citation_wrong: 1            # FIND-01 Foerster date 1995 should be 1993
  contamination: 0
  fabrication: 0               # Antell flagged as UNVERIFIED rather than FABRICATION pending verification
  silent_bridges: 0
  marker_issues: 0             # all three APPROPRIATION-FLAG markers schema-conformant; INFERENCE and SPECULATION schema-conformant
  glossary_violations: 0       # chaos-X compounds throughout body prose are pervasively in argument-context describing the framework being refused, consistent with Ballentine-critique register; see NOTE-02
  high_findings: 0
  medium_findings: 2
  low_findings: 2
  notes: 3
  total_findings: 7

# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# SUPPORTED CLAIMS — summary register
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Of 53 paragraph-claims, 51 are SUPPORTED: each EVIDENCE token routes
# through a source_id that is registered in sources.yaml (all 13 unique
# IDs verified present); each footnote attribution fairly characterises
# the cited scholar's published position; each forward-link to a
# comparative chapter (C1-C7 → Ch34-Ch40) matches toc.yaml; each back-
# link to a Part I chapter (Ch27, Ch29, Ch30, Ch31, Ch32) matches
# toc.yaml.
#
# §I Rainbow Serpent (lines 70-99) compression matches the brief's
# comparative_argument at every tested point: Radcliffe-Brown 1926
# *JRAI* 56 article "The Rainbow-Serpent Myth of Australia" as the
# pan-Aboriginal consolidation; Yingarna (western Arnhem Land),
# Ngalyod (central and eastern Arnhem Land), Wanambi (central Australia),
# Wagyl (southwest Nyungar country), Yulunggul (northeastern Arnhem
# Land Yolngu Wagilag Sisters cycle) as the regionally distinct
# ancestral beings; Merlan 1998's *Caging the Rainbow* (chs. 1-3) as
# the principal English-language area-specialist anchor for the
# consolidation-dismantling reading; Morphy 1991 / Rose 1996 / Keen
# 1994 as the wider Aboriginal-Australian area-specialist tradition
# (named-but-flagged in body prose; not currently in sources.yaml).
# The Wagilag Sisters narrative-and-ritual cycle's association with
# Yulunggul and Yolngu tradition matches Keen 1994's published
# position. The cosmographic-and-iconographic features (serpentine
# form; association with water and the rainbow; creative-cosmogonic
# role) are correctly registered as the surface-similarities that
# the consolidation pattern-matched on but that the regional
# specificity dismantles.
#
# §II Nanabozho / Mishipeshu / Thunderers (lines 101-136) compression
# matches Ch32 claims-approved at every tested point: the Anishinaabe
# trickster-culture-hero spelling variants (Nanabush, Wenebojo,
# Wisakedjak, Manabozho); Hallowell 1960's "Ojibwa Ontology, Behavior,
# and World View" essay in *Culture in History* ed. Diamond (Columbia
# UP); the "other-than-human persons" framework; the Berens River
# fieldwork of the 1930s in northwestern Ontario; the manitouk on the
# same ontological standing as human persons; the relational-and-
# reciprocity-grounded moral framework; Smith 1995's *The Island of
# the Anishnaabeg* (University of Idaho Press); the Thunderer /
# Underwater-manitou dialectic; the layered cosmographic equilibrium;
# Mishipeshu's serpentine-and-feline form; the Agawa Rock pictographs
# at Lake Superior Provincial Park; the "Underwater Panther" / "Great
# Lynx" English-language glosses; copper-related associations;
# residence in the underwater-and-subterranean realm; Vizenor 1989's
# "Trickster Discourse" essay in *Narrative Chance* (UNM Press); the
# methodological refusal to subordinate Nanabozho to Western combat-
# myth or hero/villain typologies; the Anishinaabe refusal as at the
# level of the categorical apparatus itself.
#
# §III Mwindo / Kirimu (lines 138-168) compression matches Ch29
# claims-approved at every tested point: 1956 Bese recording from
# Shé-Kárisi Candi Rureke at Bese on the upper Oso river in the
# Kivu region of what was then the eastern Belgian Congo; Biebuyck
# and Mateene 1969's *The Mwindo Epic from the Banyanga (Zaire)*
# (University of California Press) as the standard English-language
# critical edition and translation; Belcher 1999's *Epic Traditions
# of Africa* (Indiana UP) ch. on Mwindo for the maturation-through-
# humiliation pattern; the hero's biographical-and-moral arc; the
# killing of Kirimu as hubristic over-reach; Nkuba's lightning-
# seizing as the narrative's own moral correction; the celestial-
# realm trials and humiliations as morally-motivated correction;
# the conditions imposed on Mwindo's return as the content of the
# epic's pedagogy; the renunciation-of-over-reach as a specific
# moral discipline; the chiefly office's contingency on the
# celestial powers' authority. The §III treatment correctly
# develops the *fuller* methodological implication beyond Ch40 §II's
# moral-valence-inversion observation: that the Nyanga pedagogy is
# specifically Nyanga and that the comparativist's task is to
# register it in its own terms.
#
# §IV Lönnrot / Iku-Turso (lines 170-199) compression matches Ch31
# claims-approved at every tested point: Lönnrot 1802-1884; the
# Vanha Kalevala 1835 and the Uusi Kalevala 1849; Lönnrot's eleven
# major collecting tours between 1828 and 1844; the Suomalaisen
# Kirjallisuuden Seura (SKS, founded 1831); the older rune-tradition
# material as laconic, fragmentary, regionally variable across
# Karelian and Finnish singing traditions, and not synthesised into
# a single connected narrative within the singing tradition itself;
# the Iku-Turso connected-episode structure as Lönnrot's compositional
# achievement; the *Kalevala*'s textual status as closer to a
# critical edition with extensive editorial intervention than to a
# faithful transcription; the historical-political function in the
# construction of a Finnish national epic under Russian-imperial
# rule (1809-1917 Grand Duchy of the Russian Empire). One name-
# attestation issue: "Henrik Reinhold Antell" listed at line 178
# among the named rune-collectors does not appear in Ch31's
# collector-list (Lönnrot, Europaeus, Ahlqvist, Genetz, Krohn brothers)
# or anywhere else in the project's source-base (sources.yaml,
# glossary.yaml, briefs/, chapters/); see FIND-02 below.
#
# §V Bakunawa / Mapuche (lines 201-229) compression matches Ch30
# and Ch27 claims-approved at every tested point: Visayan and Bikol
# Philippine oral tradition; the seven-original-moons aetiology;
# six devoured / one survives; eclipse-aetiology as the narrative's
# load-bearing function; communal noise-making during eclipses (drums,
# gongs, shouting, metal pots per Ch30; Ch41 line 209 adds "firing
# of guns in later periods" as a minor elaboration not directly
# attested by Ch30, see FIND-03); driving-off-not-slaying; absence-of-
# a-hero; Eugenio 2001's *Philippine Folk Literature: The Myths* (UP
# of the Philippines) as the principal Philippine-language-mediated
# source; the inclusion-marginal classification per Ch30 brief; the
# Mapuche Kai-Kai vs. Tren-Tren material per Koessler-Ilg 1954's
# *Cuentan los araucanos*; the Wenu Mapu / Naq Mapu / Minche Mapu
# vertical cosmography per Foerster 1993's *Introducción a la
# religiosidad mapuche*. One date drift: Ch41 line 221 footnote
# misdates Foerster's volume as 1995 instead of 1993 (sources.yaml
# foerster-religiosidad-mapuche year=1993; Ch27 confirms 1993);
# see FIND-01 below.
#
# §VI What the refusal teaches (lines 231-263) is correctly anchored
# on Ballentine 2015 (over-extension critique) and on the chapter's
# own §§I-V evidence; the three methodological-typological lessons
# are derived from the case-set; the [SPECULATION:] marker at line
# 245 conforms to the locked schema (claim | basis | counterargument)
# with the counterargument sub-field substantively engaging the
# elastic-typology defense (combat-and-encounter-myth with both
# adversarial and reciprocal-counterpart subtypes) — consistent with
# the Ch40 [SPECULATION:] schema improvement and faithful to the
# chapter's argument structure. The closing observation that the
# inversion problem (Ch40) and the appropriation problem (Ch41) are
# the same problem viewed at different levels of the typology's
# reach is registered explicitly per Ch40 §VI.
#
# Source-ID registry check (all 13 unique IDs verified present in
# sources.yaml registry):
#   ballentine-2015, forsyth-1987, koessler-ilg-cuentan, foerster-
#   religiosidad-mapuche, biebuyck-mateene-mwindo, eugenio-philippine-
#   myths, siikala-finnish, belcher-epic, hallowell-ojibwa-world,
#   johnston-manitous, smith-island-anishnaabeg, vizenor-narrative-
#   chance, merlan-caging-rainbow.
# All resolve. No invented or unresolved IDs. (johnston-ojibway-
# heritage is registered in sources.yaml and named in body prose at
# §II but is not the route for any EVIDENCE token in Ch41; this is
# defensible since Smith 1995 / Vizenor 1989 / Hallowell 1960 /
# Johnston 1995 carry the substantive EVIDENCE-routing burden at §II.)
#
# Brief anchor-source routing check (all four brief sources.anchor
# entries correctly routed):
#   merlan-caging-rainbow → §I (lines 45, 49, 59, 73, 76, 79, 85, 91)
#   hallowell-ojibwa-world → §II (lines 41, 104, 107, 125)
#   biebuyck-mateene-mwindo → §III (lines 42, 141, 144, 147, 153, 156, 159)
#   siikala-finnish → §IV (lines 46, 173, 176, 179, 182, 185, 188, 193, 196)
# All resolve to the correct sections. PASS.
#
# COMPARATIVE-HOOK chapter-number registry check (all 17 hooks
# resolved against toc.yaml):
#   C1=Ch34, C2=Ch35, C3=Ch36, C5=Ch38, C7=Ch40 (forward-links /
#   back-links within Part II as appropriate). All resolve.
#
# APPROPRIATION-FLAG named_specialists check:
#   §I Rainbow Serpent (line 93): Merlan (in sources.yaml as
#     merlan-caging-rainbow) named correctly as principal anchor;
#     Morphy 1991, Rose 1996, Keen 1994 named-but-flagged with
#     explicit "not currently in sources.yaml" qualifier; Radcliffe-
#     Brown 1926 named as the originator of the consolidation
#     dismantled by the area-specialists. PASS.
#   §II Nanabozho (line 130): Hallowell (in sources.yaml as
#     hallowell-ojibwa-world), Johnston (johnston-ojibway-heritage,
#     johnston-manitous), Smith (smith-island-anishnaabeg), Vizenor
#     (vizenor-narrative-chance) all named correctly with sources.yaml
#     IDs explicitly indicated. Consistent with Ch32 named-specialist
#     precedent. PASS.
#   §III Mwindo (line 162): Biebuyck and Mateene (biebuyck-mateene-
#     mwindo) named correctly; Belcher (belcher-epic) named correctly;
#     Biebuyck's later *Hero and Chief* (1978) named-but-flagged per
#     Ch29 precedent. Consistent with Ch29 / Ch40 §II named-specialist
#     precedent. PASS.
# Note: §IV (Lönnrot) and §V (Bakunawa) do not have APPROPRIATION-FLAG
# markers — §IV is handled with an [INFERENCE:] marker at line 190
# (since the appropriation is by a known historical figure, not an
# area-specialist refusal), and §V's inclusion-marginal classification
# does not require an APPROPRIATION-FLAG marker (since the case is
# included on hedged terms rather than refused). This appears to be a
# deliberate and defensible design choice consistent with the brief's
# semantics; see NOTE-01 below.
#
# Section weight check:
#   Source overview (lines 35-69)        : ~9 paragraph-claims
#   §I Rainbow Serpent (70-99)           : ~7 paragraph-claims
#   §II Nanabozho/Mishipeshu (101-136)   : ~9 paragraph-claims (slight main-weight)
#   §III Mwindo (138-168)                : ~7 paragraph-claims
#   §IV Lönnrot (170-199)                : ~7 paragraph-claims
#   §V Bakunawa/Mapuche (201-229)        : ~6 paragraph-claims
#   §VI What teaches (231-263)           : ~7 paragraph-claims
# Section weights are roughly balanced, with §II Nanabozho slightly
# heavier per the brief's framing of the Anishinaabe case as "the
# principled refusal" / "the most principled refusal in the present
# book". The brief does not specify a main-weight discipline as Ch40
# does for Bida/Mwindo, so the balanced distribution is appropriate.
# PASS.
#
# Area-specialist-first discipline check (per scope.md §"Appropriation
# protocol" and brief special_instructions, the most rigorous check
# in the audit since this is the most appropriation-sensitive chapter
# in the book):
#   §I (Rainbow Serpent): The section opens at line 70 with the
#     identification of the consolidation as a 20th-century scholarly
#     construct (Radcliffe-Brown 1926) dismantled by Merlan 1998 et
#     al.; the chaoskampf-typological extension is invoked only at
#     line 87, after five paragraphs of Aboriginal-Australian
#     regional-specificity development. Radcliffe-Brown / Morphy /
#     Rose / Keen all named in body prose. PASS.
#   §II (Nanabozho): The section opens at line 103 with the
#     Anishinaabe ontological framework presented through Hallowell
#     1960 / Johnston / Smith 1995 / Vizenor 1989 on the tradition's
#     own terms (the "other-than-human persons" framework; the

#     invoked only at line 115 within an explicit "would, on a

#     class member, but the area-specialist scholarship... explicitly

#     area-specialist priority is honoured at every paragraph).
#   §III (Mwindo): The section opens at line 140 with the Nyanga
#     oral epic and its 1956 Bese recording from Shé-Kárisi Candi
#     Rureke; Biebuyck and Mateene 1969 + Belcher 1999 are the

#   §V (Bakunawa / Mapuche): The section opens at line 203 with the

findings:
  - id: FIND-01
    severity: MEDIUM
    category: citation-wrong | date-error
    claim_anchor: line 221 (§V, Mapuche supporting case — footnote citation of Foerster's volume)
    description: |
      Ch41 line 221's body-prose footnote cites Foerster's volume as
      "Foerster, R., *Introducción a la religiosidad mapuche*
      (Santiago: Editorial Universitaria, 1995)". The correct
      publication date is 1993, per (a) sources.yaml line 557 entry
      foerster-religiosidad-mapuche year: 1993; (b) the full_citation
      field at line 558: "Foerster González, R., *Introducción a la
      religiosidad mapuche* (Santiago: Editorial Universitaria,
      1993)."; (c) Ch27 claims-approved line 39's author-year "Foerster

summary: |
  Ch41 is the most methodologically-sensitive chapter in the book per
  brief special_instructions and the closing comparative chapter of
  Part II, drawing on five Part-I anchor chapters (Ch32 Nanabozho/
  Mishipeshu, Ch29 Mwindo/Kirimu, Ch31 Väinämöinen/Iku-Turso, Ch30
  Bakunawa, Ch27 Mapuche Kai-Kai/Tren-Tren) and on the held-for-this-
  chapter Rainbow Serpent critique, with two methodological-typological
  comparative anchors (Forsyth 1987, Ballentine 2015). All 13 unique
  source_ids resolve in sources.yaml (ballentine-2015, forsyth-1987,
  koessler-ilg-cuentan, foerster-religiosidad-mapuche, biebuyck-mateene-
  mwindo, eugenio-philippine-myths, siikala-finnish, belcher-epic,
  hallowell-ojibwa-world, johnston-manitous, smith-island-anishnaabeg,
  vizenor-narrative-chance, merlan-caging-rainbow); all 17 COMPARATIVE-
  HOOK chapter-references resolve in toc.yaml. The brief's four
  sources.anchor entries are all correctly routed (merlan-caging-
  rainbow → §I, hallowell-ojibwa-world → §II, biebuyck-mateene-mwindo
  → §III, siikala-finnish → §IV). The three [APPROPRIATION-FLAG:]
  markers (§I, §II, §III) all conform to the locked four-sub-field
  schema (substance | named_specialists | tier_implication |
  forwards_to) — sub-field conformance is a clean PASS at all three
  loci, with named-specialists correctly distinguishing in-sources.yaml
  anchors (Merlan, Hallowell, Johnston, Smith, Vizenor, Biebuyck,
  Mateene, Belcher) from named-but-flagged secondary anchors
  (Radcliffe-Brown 1926, Morphy 1991, Rose 1996, Keen 1994; Biebuyck
  *Hero and Chief* 1978). The §IV [INFERENCE:] marker (line 190) and
  §VI [SPECULATION:] marker (line 245) both conform to their locked
  schemas, with the SPECULATION marker's counterargument sub-field
  substantively engaging the elastic-typology defense. Area-specialist-
  first discipline status: CLEAN PASS at all five sections — every
  Anishinaabe / Aboriginal-Australian / Nyanga claim is anchored on a
  named area specialist before any chaoskampf-typological overlay is
  invoked, and the brief's special_instructions on the appropriation-
  protocol commitment are honoured at every paragraph. Two MEDIUM
  findings: FIND-01 date error in line 221 footnote (Foerster's volume
  is 1993, not 1995 as cited; the EVIDENCE token at line 223 is
  correctly source_id=foerster-religiosidad-mapuche so the attribution
  is unaffected, but the bibliographic citation is wrong); FIND-02
  unverified scholar name "Henrik Reinhold Antell" at line 178 in the
  list of named rune-collectors (no upstream attestation in Ch31, in
  sources.yaml, or anywhere else in the project source-base; the name
  does not match any known 19th-century Finnish rune-collector that
  the auditor can verify, and the chapter should not assert the
  participation of a named historical figure without an upstream
  anchor). Two LOW findings (FIND-03 minor narrative-detail elaboration
  about gun-firing in Bakunawa eclipse-noise practices at line 209,
  not directly attested by Ch30; FIND-04 minor name-form discipline
  drift at line 221 — "Rolf Foerster" / "Foerster, R." rather than
  the canonical "Rolf Foerster González" / "Foerster González, R.")
  and three NOTE-level positive structural observations (NOTE-01 the
  marker-distribution choice — three APPROPRIATION-FLAG at §§I-III
  plus one INFERENCE at §IV plus no marker at §V — is well-aligned to
  the brief's semantics; NOTE-02 the chapter-wide "chaos-X" compounds
  are pervasively in argument-context describing the framing being
  refused, consistent with the chapter's critique-register argument
  structure and not a glossary violation; NOTE-03 the named-but-
  flagged pattern is a model application). Most consequential single
  finding: FIND-02, the unverified "Henrik Reinhold Antell"
  attribution, which is the only instance in the chapter at which a
  named historical figure is asserted without an upstream anchor or
  external-source verification — particularly notable in a chapter
  whose central methodological commitment is the area-specialist
  citation discipline. FIND-01 (the Foerster date error) is also
  consequential because it appears in a chapter that explicitly
  emphasises area-specialist citation discipline. Per the verdict
  rule "PASS if zero HIGH and ≤3 MEDIUM; otherwise REVISE", with 2
  MEDIUM findings the chapter is below the threshold for REVISE on
  count alone, but both MEDIUM findings are bibliographic-attribution
  errors in a chapter whose central methodological commitment is the
  area-specialist citation discipline, and the conservative reading
  is REVISE because (a) FIND-02's unverified name is the single most
